}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 0 L 1.28 1.11 L 3.84 -1.11 L 6.4 1.11 L 7.68 0 L 7.68 0 L 7.7 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.66763pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.49 0 L 0.28 1.88 L 0 0 L 0.28 -1.88 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{r}:k\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{q}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
by well-nestedness of
\mathtt{G}
. The case is similar to the second base case for
\mathbf{C}=[\,\_\,]
.

•

Inductive case
\mathbf{C}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{q}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[\,\_\,]
. Similar to the above.

•

Inductive cases
\mathtt{G}=[\,\_\,]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}
or
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}[\,\_\,]
: the move can only be by
[\mathtt{Inst}]
which does not change the structure of the process, hence done.

•

Inductive case
\mathtt{G}=\mu\mathtt{t}.[\,\_\,]
: the move can only be by
[\mathtt{Rec}]
, and by its premise the type in the hole preserves well-nestedness. The thesis is by induction.

The case for Definition
D.3
(2) is similar to the one for Definition
D.3
(1): either
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
with some participants on the right-hand side of the MC, or the next committing role is included in
\mathcal{R}
by
\mathtt{[Rsnd]}
.
∎

D.5.
Preservation of awareness

Awareness is preserved by transition. Before proving this property we give an auxiliary proposition.
Proposition

D.5
states that if all roles in
\mathtt{G}
depend on
\mathtt{p}
then the first action of
\mathtt{G}
is an action by
\mathtt{p}
.

Proposition D.5.

If
\mathtt{p}<_{\mathtt{G}}
and
\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\
then
\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{p}
.

Proposition D.6 (Awareness Preservation).

\mathtt{G}\text{ is aware }\penalty\ \land\penalty\ \mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}\text{ is aware. }

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation, proceeding by case analysis on the last rule used.

Base cases

If the transition is by
\mathtt{[Snd]}
, any MC in any continuation
\mathtt{G}_{i}
, which is aware in
\mathtt{G}
by hypothesis, remains unchanged hence aware. The case for
\mathtt{[Rcv]}
is similar. The case for
\mathtt{[Inst]}
only changes the outer MC definition into an active MC with empty L-set and R-set. If L-set and R-set are empty awareness for MC definitions is equivalent to awareness for active MC. The thesis is therefore straightforward by awareness of
\mathtt{G}
.

Inductive cases

Cases for
[\mathtt{Cont1}]
and
[\mathtt{Cont2}]
are straightforward by induction.

For
[\mathtt{Rec}]
, observe that a recursive type has a transition
\ell
if and only if its one-time unfolding has a transition
\ell
, and they both reach the same state. Therefore, if
\mathtt{G}=\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}^{\prime\prime}
is aware then also
\mathtt{G}^{\prime\prime}\,[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}^{\prime\prime}/\mathtt{t}]
is aware and after a transition they reach the same state
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
. By inductive hypothesis and awareness of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime\prime}\,[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}^{\prime\prime}/\mathtt{t}]
it follows that
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is aware hence done.

For
[\mathtt{Ctx1}]
then
\mathtt{G}={\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\penalty\ }_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{r}}}
and by induction if
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}
makes a move to
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
then
\mathtt{G}_{l}^{\prime}
is aware. So
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{r}}
are aware (the second directly by hypothesis) meaning that all MC in them are aware. It remains to show that the outermost MC
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\penalty\ }_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{r}}
is still aware after the transition.

This follows by the fact that the move with label
\nu c,n
leaves LR-sets, and participants and dependencies unchanged from
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}
to
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
. The symmetric case is similar.

For
[\mathtt{LSnd}]
assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{{\mathtt{l}}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\penalty\ }_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{r}

and
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\penalty\ }_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{r}
and the observer of
\mathtt{G}
is
\mathtt{p}
.
Single decision and clear termination of
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}
follow by induction and awareness of
\mathtt{G}_{r}
follows by hypothesis. Single decision of
\mathtt{G}
follows by single decision of
\mathtt{G}_{\mathtt{l}}
and by the fact that
\mathtt{G}_{r}
is unchanged by the transition. We only need to show clear termination of the outermost MC in
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
.
If
\mathcal{L}\not=\emptyset
then the outermost MC in
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is clear termination since the premise in the implication of case (2) of Definition
3.9
is negative.
Assume now
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
. By clear-termination of
\mathtt{G}
at least one of the following holds: (a) all roles diverge in
\mathtt{G}_{1}
, (b) every state reached by
\mathtt{G}_{1}
and any role in
\mathtt{G}_{1}
, it is possible to reach a state where that roles makes an action.
(a) is clearly preserved by reduction (an infinite execution remains infinite if we remove a finite prefix). (b) is also preserved since rule
[\mathtt{LSnd}]
does not change
\mathcal{L}
and hence future committing action that makes
\mathtt{G}_{1}
clear-termination is also available in
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
.

For
[\mathtt{LRcv1}]
assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{l}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{r}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{r}
and the reached state has type
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}_{l}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{r}}_{\mathcal{L}\cup\{\mathtt{q}\},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{r}
.
To show clear-termination, observe that the reached state has L-set
\mathcal{L}\cup\{\mathtt{q}\}\not=\emptyset
and hence clear-termination of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
follows immediately (by negative premise).
Single decision is trivial since the right-hand side of the MC remains unchanged (hence still enjoys single decision).

Case
[\mathtt{LRcv2}]
(single-decision and clear-termination) is similar to
[\mathtt{LSnd}]
except we now know that the action does not have the observer as subject by premise of the rule
[\mathtt{LRcv2}]
.

Cases
[\mathtt{RSnd}]
and
[\mathtt{RRcv}]
: clear termination follows by hypothesis since the left-hand side of the MC does not change, single decision follows by negative premise since the L-set in the reached state includes the subject of
\ell
and hence is not empty.
∎

D.6.
Preservation of balance

Balance is not, in general, preserved by transition. For example, the global type on the left of (
5
) is balanced and it reduces to a state, on the right of (
5
), that is not since
\mathtt{q}
is nor in the roles of the LHS nor in the left commit set.

(5)

\mathtt{q}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{p}:a_{k}.\mathtt{end}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\mathtt{p}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{q}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}}{{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}
}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 0 L 1.28 1.11 L 3.84 -1.11 L 6.4 1.11 L 7.68 0 L 7.68 0 L 7.7 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.66763pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.49 0 L 0.28 1.88 L 0 0 L 0.28 -1.88 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{p}:k\,a_{k}.\mathtt{end}\,\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\mathtt{p}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}

However, in presence of awareness, balance is preserved by transition.

Proposition D.7 (Balance & Preservation).

\mathtt{G}\text{ is aware and balanced }\penalty\ \land\penalty\ \mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}\text{ is balanced. }

The proof of Proposition
D.7
is mechanical by induction on the transition. One key point to observe is that clear-termination always ensures that any role
\mathtt{r}
not yet committed in a MC still appears in the LHS (either because it has to receive a committing message, or because it diverges in the LHS). In the first case each role will naturally occur until it is committed, in the second case it will occur in the unfolding. This is key to preserve balance.

D.7.
Coherence

Definition D.8 (Coherent
\mathtt{G}
).

\mathtt{G}
is
coherent
if:
\mathtt{G}=\mathbf{C}[\,\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\,]\,\implies\,\mathcal{L}=\emptyset\,\lor\,\mathcal{R}=\emptyset

Informally, coherence of
\mathtt{G}
requires that no role is committed to different sides of the same active MC in
\mathtt{G}
. Coherence is preserved by transition and hence is invariant for all states reachable from an initial global types enjoying balance and awareness.

The proof of coherence is given after a few auxiliaries.

Definition D.9 (Ready roles).

We say that a role is
ready
in
\mathtt{G}
, written
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{ready}(\mathtt{G})
, if there exists
\ell
such that
\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\
and
\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{p}
.

Namely, a role
\mathtt{p}
is ready in
\mathtt{G}
if

\mathtt{p}
can immediately make a send or receive action.

The following proposition can be proved mechanically by induction on the syntax of
\mathtt{G}_{1}
, observing that the definition of committing set always adds the observer of a MC first, to the committing set of a mixed choice, before all other causally related actions.

Proposition D.10.

Given a MC
\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
with observer
\mathtt{p}
and (finite or infinite) execution
\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\vec{l}}}\penalty\
, the first committing action in vector
\ell
(if any is committing) has subject
\mathtt{p}
.

Proposition D.11 (LR-initiation).

Consider an
aware
active MC of the form
\mathbf{C}[\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}]

(1)

If
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{ready}(\mathtt{G}_{2})\setminus\{\mathtt{p}\}
then
\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{R}
.

(2)

If
\mathtt{p}\not\in\mathcal{R}
then
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
.

(3)

If
\mathtt{p}\not\in\mathcal{L}
then
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

(sketch)
To prove (1,2) observe that the execution of any good state
\mathtt{G}
is in one of the following four meta-states:

(1)

(initial):
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{G}
and
\mathtt{G}
has no active MC instances
c,n
;

(2)

(initialized)
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{G}
;

(3)

(R-committed)
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}\cup\{\mathtt{p}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
.

In state (1)
\mathtt{G}
can make:

•

transitions that are not with label
\nu c,n^{\prime}
with
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. This can be by
[\mathtt{Snd}]
,
[\mathtt{Rcv}]
,
[\mathtt{Cont1}]
,
[\mathtt{Cont2}]
,
[\mathtt{Rec}]
that will preserve state (1),

•

a transition with label
\nu c,n^{\prime}
with
n^{\prime}<n
and move to state (1),

•

a transition with label
\nu c,n
and move to state (2).

In state (2)
\mathtt{G}
can make

•

non-committing actions by
[\mathtt{LSnd}]
,
[\mathtt{LRcv2}]
,
[\mathtt{RTAct}]
that preserve state (2),

•

an action committing on the left by
[\mathtt{LRcv1}]
which also preserves state (2),

•

an action committing on the right. Since
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
and the first committing action on the RHS is by the observer (by
awareness - single decision
)
hence reaches a state with subterm
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\{\mathtt{p}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
and which is in state (3).

Any transition from (3) lead to state (3) by
monotonicity
(
Proposition

D.2
).

The case (3) that
\mathtt{p}\not\in\mathcal{L}
implies
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
can be proved similarly with meta states

(1)

(initial):
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{G}
but
\mathtt{G}
has no MC
c,n
;

(2)

(initialized)
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\emptyset,\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{G}
;

(3)

(L-committed)
{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L}\cup\{\mathtt{p}\},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}
.

In this case the only committing action on the left-hand side (main block) would be for rule
[\mathtt{LRcv1}]
from meta-state (2) to meta-state (3) which is the only rule allowing for a committing action (i.e., altering
\mathcal{L}
).
By Proposition
D.10
this action is by the observer.
Preservation of meta-state (3) is by
monotonicity
(
Proposition

D.2
).
∎

Proposition

D.11
shows that the observer of a MC (1) is always the one making the first action on the RHS and (2,3) is always the first one to commit to any side.

Lemma D.12 (Coherence Preservation).

\mathtt{G}\text{ is coherent and aware }\land\penalty\ \mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\text{ is coherent}

Proof.

Coherence relies on the dependency between actions guaranteed by awareness.

Let
\mathtt{G}=\mathbf{C}[\,\mathtt{G}_{h}\,]
for some
\mathtt{G}_{h}
. We proceed by induction on the syntax of
\mathbf{C}
and inner induction on the syntax of
\mathtt{G}_{h}
.

Case
\mathbf{C}=[\,\_\,]
(base case outer induction).

In this case
\mathtt{G}_{h}=\mathtt{G}
.
If
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{end}
the thesis is immediate.
If
\mathtt{G}
is a communication the thesis follow by inner induction on
\mathtt{G}
.
If
\mathtt{G}
is a recursion then action
\ell
is by rule
\mathtt{[Rec]}
and
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}={\mathtt{G}^{\prime}}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}/\mathtt{t}]
.
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is coherent by inner induction, and
\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}
is coherent by hypothesis. It follows that
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is coherent, hence done.
The interesting case is for

\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}

We proceed by case analysis on the last rule used to derive transition.
The last rule applied is one of the following:

•

\mathtt{[Snd]}
or
\mathtt{[Rcv]}
: the thesis follows by the coherence hypothesis and the fact that
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
are not modified by these rules.

•

\mathtt{[Inst]}
,
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
or
\mathtt{[Ctx2]}
:
These rules do not change
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
so in case of
\mathtt{[Inst]}
the thesis is immediate, in case of
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
and
\mathtt{[Ctx2]}
it is directly by induction.

•

\mathtt{[LSnd]}
or
\mathtt{[LRcv2]}
:
\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
with
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
coherent by (inner) induction. The transition does not change
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{R}
, and
\mathtt{G}_{2}
. Then
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
is also coherent, hence done.

•

\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
:
\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{s}\mathtt{r}?a}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
with
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
coherent by (inner) induction and
a
committing in
\mathtt{G}
. Since
a
is a committing receive action, the corresponding send action is also committing, the We have two cases:

–

if
\mathtt{s}\in\mathcal{L}
then
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
by coherence of
\mathtt{G}
.

–

if
\mathtt{s}\not\in\mathcal{L}
then
\mathtt{s}=\mathtt{p}
(i.e.,
\mathtt{s}
is the observer). By premise of
\mathtt{[LRcv1]}

\mathtt{s}\not in\mathtt{r}
and by
Proposition

D.11
–
LR-initiation
(2) if the observer is not in
\mathcal{R}
then
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
.

In either of the cases above
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
, hence
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L}\cup\{\mathtt{q}\},\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
is coherent.

•

\mathtt{[RSnd]}
: in this case

\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}\cup\{\mathtt{r}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
. Since the rule adds an element to
\mathcal{R}
we need to show that
\mathcal{L}
is empty.
We have two cases:

–

if
\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{p}
, since
\mathtt{p}\not\in\mathcal{L}
(by premise of
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
) then
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
(by Lemma
D.11
(3))

–

if
\mathtt{r}\not=\mathtt{p}
then since
\mathtt{r}
is R-acting we have
\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{R}
(by Lemma
D.11
(1)). By coherence of
\mathtt{G}
and
\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{R}
it follows
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
.

Since
\mathcal{L}
is unchanged in
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
then
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is coherent.

•

\mathtt{[RRcv]}
: Similar to the case for
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
.

•

\mathtt{[RTAct]}
:
\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
. Since
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
are coherent by induction and
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
remain unchanged, then

\mathtt{G}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
is also coherent.

•

\mathtt{[Cont1]}
or
\mathtt{[Cont2]}
: immediate by (inner) induction.

Case
\mathbf{C}
is a prefix.

If
\mathbf{C}
is a prefix, either communication or message in transit, the transition of
\mathtt{G}
is by
\mathtt{[Snd]}
or
\mathtt{[Rcv]}
, this case is similar to the first case for
\mathbf{C}=[\,\_\,]
. The case for transition by
\mathtt{[Cont1]}
or
\mathtt{[Cont2]}
is also similar, by inner induction.

Case
\mathbf{C}=[\,\_\,]\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{r}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
.

By hypothesis, either
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
or
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
. If
By Lemma
D.4
(
well-nestedness
) the form of
\mathbf{C}
can be assumed to be
\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}\cup\{a.[\,\_\,]\}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
.
Proceed by case analysis on the last rule used in the derivation:

•

\mathtt{[Lsnd]}
: in the premise, either
\mathbf{C}
moves by
\mathtt{[Lsnd]}
or
{\mathtt{G}}
(the global type in the hole) moves. In the first case, only the MCs in the selected branch appear in the context after transition and, in those MCs, the sets do not change. The thesis then follows by coherence of
\mathtt{G}
. If
\mathtt{G}_{h}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{h}^{\prime}
by inner induction
\mathtt{G}_{h}^{\prime}
is coherent, hence, since
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{R}
, and
\mathtt{G}_{2}
are coherent by hypothesis, also
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is coherent.

•

\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
: if
\mathbf{C}
moves by
\mathtt{[Snd]}
this case is similar to the one above for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
.

•

\mathtt{[LRcv2]}
: Similar to
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
.

•

\mathtt{[RSnd]}
: since this rules adds
\mathtt{q}
to
\mathcal{L}
by
Proposition

D.11
–
LR-initiation
(3) – it must be
\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{L}
and hence
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
for coherence of
\mathtt{G}
.

•

\mathtt{[RRcv]}
: This rule adds an element of the right-hand side set.
By
Proposition

D.11
–
LR-initiation
(1) if
\mathcal{R}
is not empty then
\mathtt{q}\in\mathcal{R}
hence
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
.

Case
\mathbf{C}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{r}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S}\cup\{a.[\,\_\,]\}
.

Similar to the symmetric one.

Case
\mathbf{C}=[\,\_\,]\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
or

\mathbf{C}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c:\mathtt{p}}\mkern 1.0mu}[\,\_\,]

. Immediate by hypothesis since
\mathtt{G}
only allows an action by [Inst] which does not change
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
.

Case
\mu\mathtt{t}.[\,\_\,]

By induction.

∎

Next, Since initial states are coherent, then all states reachable from an initial one are coherent.

Corollary D.13 (Coherence Invariant).

Any
\mathtt{G}
reachable from an initial global type that enjoys balance and awareness is coherent.

D.8.
Progress

Progress is proved by first looking at single steps (
Lemma

D.14
). The diagram below gives a highlight of how the auxiliary lemmata and definitions contribute to
Lemma

D.14
. The diagram outlines the dependencies of progress from the definitions (shown in blue) and lemmata given before.

The final result
Theorem

3.13
follows from
Lemma

D.14
observing that initial well set global types are aware and balanced, and that these properties are preserved by transition.

Lemma D.14 (Progress).

If
\mathtt{G}
is aware, balanced, and coherent then
\mathtt{G}
enjoys progress.

Proof.

We proceed by case analysis on the syntax of
\mathtt{G}
.

If
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
since
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
then either
\mathtt{r}\in\{\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\}
or
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{G}_{i}
for all
i
(by balance of
\mathtt{G}
).
If
\mathtt{r}\in\{\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\}
then the thesis holds immediately after either a step
!\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}a_{i}

?\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}a_{i}

The case for

is similar.

If
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c,n:\mathtt{p}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
, by
coherence
of
\mathtt{G}
(hypothesis) we can assume
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
or
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
. So we have three cases.

(1)

assume
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset\land\mathcal{R}\neq\emptyset
.
Since
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
then

(7)

\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}\cup\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{2})\setminus\emptyset\qquad\text{(by definition of $\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})$)}

and

(8)

\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{1})\cup\emptyset=\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{2})\cup\mathcal{R}\qquad\text{(by {balance})}

By combining (
7
) and (
8
) we know

\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{2})
.

Observe that
\mathtt{G}_{2}
is participating, aware, coherent (by inductive definitions, and universal quantification of awareness over all MCs) and hence, by induction,
\mathtt{G}_{2}
enjoys progress:

\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \hskip-5.69046pt\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \text{ with }\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{r}

We need to check that all actions of
\mathtt{G}_{2}
that bring to action
\ell
, and
\ell
itself, can also be executed by the MC. By inspection of the rules, actions on the right-hand side of the MC can be lifted to the MC context
using one of the following rules:
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
,
\mathtt{[RRcv]}
, or
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
.
Rule
\mathtt{[RRcv]}
can only be applied. Rules
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
and
\mathtt{[Ctx2]}
can be applied if the subject of the action is not in
\mathcal{L}
. This is the case by
coherence preservation
.

(2)

assume
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{L}\neq\emptyset
. By definition of
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
we have that
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
implies
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{1})\setminus\emptyset
or
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{2})\setminus\mathcal{L}
.
As in the symmetric case, by
balance
of
\mathtt{G}
, we can infer
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{1})
.
By coherence and balance of
\mathtt{G}
also
\mathtt{G}_{1}
is coherent, and enjoys balance.
By induction,
\mathtt{G}_{1}
enjoys progress:

\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \hskip-5.69046pt\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \text{ with }\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{r}

First observe that by
clear-termination
if
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}{\mathtt{G}_{2}}
then it is also in and

To lift each action of
\mathtt{G}_{1}
to
\mathtt{G}
we need to use, for each transition, one of the rules below (as last applied rule)
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
,
\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
,
\mathtt{[LRcv2]}
, or
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
.
Rule
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
requires that the subject of the action is not in
\mathcal{R}
which holds by since
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
in
\mathtt{G}
and this is preserved by coherence since
\mathcal{L}\not=\emptyset
. Similarly,
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
requires that
\mathcal{R}
does not coincide with the set of roles of the base type which is true since
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
.

The only premise that would disallow the same transition to
\mathtt{G}
is that the subject of that action must not be in
\mathcal{R}
in
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
,
\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
, and
\mathtt{[Lrcv2]}
, which follows since in this case we assume
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
. Similarly, the premise of
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
that
\mathtt{R}(\underline{\mathtt{G}})\not=\mathcal{R}
(all roles of the base type have committed on the right), holds since
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
.

(3)

assume
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
. By
balance
of
\mathtt{G}
,
\mathtt{r}
is in both
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{1})
and
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}_{2})
.
By coherence and balance of
\mathtt{G}
also
\mathtt{G}_{1}
and
\mathtt{G}_{2}
are both coherent, and enjoy balance.
By induction, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathtt{G}_{i}
enjoys progress:

\mathtt{G}_{i}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \hskip-5.69046pt\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \text{ with }\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{r}

We have two cases: (1) all actions in
\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\
are non-committing, or (2) there is at least one committing action in
\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\
.
In case (1), the
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
of the intermediate states will be left empty, and hence all actions can be lifted to
\mathtt{G}
as the only premises for an action on the RHS (resp. LHS) is that the subject of the action is not in
\mathcal{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{R}
). Similarly for the context rules for MC initialization.
In case (2), without loss of generality, assume

\mathtt{G}_{i}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\
is broken into the sequence
\mathtt{G}_{i}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell_{c}}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\
such that all actions before
\ell_{c}
are non-committing and

\ell_{c}
is committing. Let
\mathtt{G}_{c}
be the state reached after
\ell_{c}
.
All actions before
\ell_{c}
can be lifted to
\mathtt{G}
(it can be shown using the arguments in case (1) above). Action
\ell_{c}
itself can be lifted to side
i
as the corresponding commitment set (
\mathcal{R}
or
\mathcal{L}
) is empty leading to a coherent MC where one commitment set is empty and one is the singleton
\{\mathtt{sbj}(\ell_{c})\}
. We have reduced this case to one of the cases
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset\land\mathcal{R}\neq\emptyset
or
\mathcal{L}\neq\emptyset\land\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
and the proof can be concluded using the arguments of these cases.

The other cases are either trivial or straightforward by induction.
∎

\frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1},\sigma),Y^{\prime}\end{array}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y^{\prime}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{r}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{2},\sigma),Y^{\prime}\end{array}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\bullet\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{2},\sigma),Y^{\prime}}&{\footnotesize\textsc{[LSnd/RSnd]}}\\
\\
\frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime})\end{array}}\hskip 8.04996pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0}a\in\Downarrow^{c}\hskip-2.84526pt(\underline{\mathtt{G}})}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet,\sigma^{\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime})\end{array}}\hskip 8.04996pt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0}a\not\in\Downarrow^{c}\hskip-2.84526pt(\underline{\mathtt{G}})}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma^{\prime})}&{\footnotesize\textsc{[LRcv1/2]}}\\[22.76228pt]
\frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{r}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{2},\sigma^{\prime})\end{array}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\bullet\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{2},\sigma^{\prime})}&{\footnotesize\textsc{[RRcv]}}\\[22.76228pt]
\frac{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet,\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet,\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{r}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\bullet\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\bullet\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}}&{\footnotesize\textsc{[LCtxt/RCtxt]}}\\[22.76228pt]
\frac{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime},\sigma),Y}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi.\texttt{r}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime},\sigma),Y}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime},\sigma),Y}&{\footnotesize\textsc{[NLCtxt/NRCtxt]}}\end{array}

Figure 13.
Local semantics: complete rules.

E.2.
Projection: definition of merge

The projection of a communication and message in transit relies on a merge operator. We give below the full definition of merge, which extends the standard merge to MC trivially, by using the first identity case for both MC definitions and active MC.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathtt{L}_{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}=\begin{cases}\mathtt{L}_{1}&\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}=\mathtt{L}_{2}\\[14.22636pt]
\mathtt{p}\&\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}\}_{i\in I\setminus J}\cup\{b_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{2}\}_{j\in J\setminus I}\cup\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}\}_{i\in I\cap J}&\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{L}_{1}=\mathtt{p}\&\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}\}_{i\in I},\text{ and }\\
\mathtt{L}_{2}=\mathtt{p}\&\{b_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{2}\}_{j\in J}\text{ and }\\
\forall i\in I\cap J.\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}=b_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}\end{array}\\[22.76228pt]
\mu\mathtt{t}.(\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime})&\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{L}_{i}=\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime},\penalty\ \penalty\ i\in\{1,2\}\end{array}\\[14.22636pt]
\bot&\penalty\ \text{ otherwise }\end{cases}\end{array}

Appendix F
Operational Correspondence

F.1.
Full definition of
<:

The full definition of preorder ‘
<:
’ on pairs of local types, and its lifting to configurations and systems is defined below.

\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}\hskip 8.19447pt(\forall i\in I)\hskip 8.19447pt\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,\in\{\&,\,\oplus\}}{\mathtt{p}\,\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,_{i\mathclose{\in}I}\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{p}\,\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,_{i\mathclose{\in}I}\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}}\qquad\par\frac{\mathtt{L}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{k}\hskip 8.19447ptk\in I}{\mathtt{p}\,\&_{i\mathclose{\in}I}\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{p}\,\&\,a_{k}.\mathtt{L}_{k}}\\
\\
\frac{\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}\hskip 8.19447pt(\forall i\in\{1,2\})\hskip 8.19447pt\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,\in\{\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright\mkern 1.0mu},\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\}}{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\,\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,\,\,\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{1}\,\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,\,\,\mathtt{L}_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{1}\hskip 16.38895pt\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{2}}{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\penalty\ \mathbin{<:}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}}\quad\frac{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{1}}{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet\penalty\ \mathbin{<:}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet}\quad\frac{\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{2}}{\bullet\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\penalty\ \mathbin{<:}\penalty\ \bullet\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}_{2}}\\
\\
\frac{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}}{\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}}\qquad\frac{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}}{\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}}\qquad\frac{}{\mathtt{t}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{t}}\qquad\frac{}{\mathsf{end}\mathbin{<:}\mathsf{end}}\\[14.22636pt]
\hline\cr\\
\frac{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}\hskip 8.19447pt}{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma)\mathbin{<:}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L},\sigma)}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{[PConfig]}\qquad\frac{\forall\mathtt{r}\mathop{\in}R\penalty\ \penalty\ Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}Y_{\mathtt{r}}}{\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in R}\mathbin{<:}\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}\}_{\mathtt{r}\mathclose{\in}R}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{[PSystem]}\end{array}

F.2.
Fidelity

F.2.1.
Auxiliaries

First, observe that projected local configurations do not have garbage. This simplifies our reasoning about the correspondence.

Lemma F.1.

Let
\pi_{out}\vdash\mathtt{G}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{L},\sigma
for
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
. Then for all
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G}),\,(a,\pi)\in\sigma(\mathtt{p})
,

\mathtt{stale}(\pi_{in})=\mathtt{false}
where
\pi=\pi_{out}.\pi_{in}
.

Proof.

By induction on the syntax of
\mathtt{G}
.
The base cases for
\mathtt{G}\in\{\mathtt{end},\,\mathtt{t}\}
follow by observing that forall
\pi
,
\mathtt{stale}(\pi,\mathtt{end})=\mathtt{stale}(\pi,\mathtt{t})=\mathtt{false}
.
If
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{\mathtt{p}}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{\mathtt{q}}:\{a_{i}:\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, in the first two cases of the corresponding projection rule the local type has a send or a receive prefix, hence no path is stale for them. The third case using merge follows by induction. Similarly, if
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
and
\mathtt{G}=\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
: the projection is not stale for any path.

The interesting case is the one for
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
. Since
\mathtt{L}
is a mixed choice then
\pi_{out}^{\prime}
is not empty (by projection, one side, left or right is added to the path). So, without loss of generality, assume
\pi=\pi_{out}.\mathtt{s}.\pi_{in}
with
\mathtt{s}\in\{\texttt{l},\texttt{r}\}
.
If
\mathtt{r}\in\mathcal{L}
then by induction forall
(a,\pi)
in some of
\mathtt{r}
’s queues
\sigma
, we have
\mathtt{stale}(\pi_{in},\mathtt{L}_{1})=\mathtt{false}
with
\pi=\pi_{out}.\mathtt{s}.\pi_{in}
. Since
\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet
hence
\mathtt{s}=\texttt{l}
, hence

\mathtt{stale}(\pi_{in},\mathtt{L}_{1})=\mathtt{false}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Longrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{stale}(\texttt{l}.\pi_{in},\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet)=\mathtt{false}

as desired.

The case for
\mathtt{r}\in\mathcal{L}
is symmetric to the case above for
\mathtt{r}\in\mathcal{R}
. The otherwise case for
\mathtt{r}\not\in\mathcal{L}\cup\mathcal{R}
also follows by observing that, whatever the MC side that originated the message,

\mathtt{stale}(\pi_{in},\mathtt{L}_{i})=\mathtt{false}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Longrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{stale}(\mathtt{s}_{i}.\pi_{in},\mathtt{L}_{i}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}\bullet)=\mathtt{false}

with
\mathtt{s}_{1}=\texttt{l}
and
\mathtt{s}_{2}=\texttt{r}
.
∎

Lemma F.2 (Garbageless Projections).

Let
\epsilon\vdash\mathtt{G}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{L},\sigma
for a
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
. Then

(\mathtt{r},\sigma,\mathtt{L})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{r},\sigma^{\prime},\mathtt{L}^{\prime})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \sigma=\sigma^{\prime}

Proof.

This lemma follows from
Lemma

F.1
with
\pi=\epsilon
, observing that in absence of stale messages garbage collection on
\sigma
returns
\sigma
itself.
∎

In
Proposition

F.3
(and in the rest of the paper), by projectability of
\mathtt{G}
on
\mathtt{p}
we intend the fact that the projection of
\mathtt{G}
on
\mathtt{p}
returns.
We say that
\mathtt{G}
is
projectable
if the (partial) projection function
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{p}
returns for all
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
.

Proposition F.3 (Projectability Preservation).

If
\mathtt{G}
is projectable and
{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}
then
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is projectable.

Proposition F.4.

If
\pi\vdash\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:S\upharpoonright\mathtt{q}=\mathtt{L},\sigma
then
\sigma(\mathtt{p})
is the empty message sequence
\epsilon
.

Proposition F.5 (Determinism of
\sigma
).

\mathtt{G}_{1},\sigma\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \land\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{2},\sigma\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Longrightarrow\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}

Proposition F.6.

If
\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}\sqcap\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}
is defined then
\mathtt{L}_{1}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}/\mathtt{t}]\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}/\mathtt{t}]
is defined.

Proposition F.7.

\mathtt{L}_{1}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]=\mathtt{L}_{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]
.

Proposition F.8 (Merge and
<:
).

Consider local types
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
,
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}
,
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}
,
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
. Assume that for all
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}
and
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{i}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{i}
is defined. Then

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}

Proof.

By induction on the depth of the derivation syntax of
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
.

Base cases.

There are two axioms in the judgments in
Section

F.1
, for
\mathtt{t}
and
\mathtt{end}
. If
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}=\mathtt{t}
it can only be
t<:\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}=\mathtt{t}
.
Since
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
is defined, then
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}=\mathtt{t}
and by definition of ‘
<:
’ we have
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}=\mathtt{t}
. Since
\mathtt{t}\sqcap\mathtt{t}=\mathtt{t}
the thesis is immediate by hypothesis. The case for
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}=\mathtt{end}
is similar.

Inductive cases - prefix rule.

If the
first rule
is used then
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
is either a sending or a receiving prefix. If
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}=\mathtt{p}\&_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}
then by definition of ‘
<:
’ we have

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}=\mathtt{p}\&_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}
with

(9)

\mathtt{L}_{i}<:\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\qquad\forall i\in I

Note that by ‘
<:
’ both
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}
have choices over the same set
I
.
By definedness of
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
then
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}=\mathtt{p}\&_{j\in J}a_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime}
.
By
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
we have
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}=\mathtt{p}\&_{j\in J}a_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime\prime}

with

(10)

\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}\qquad\forall j\in J

By definition of merge,
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
has the following form:

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}=\mathtt{p}\&\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus J}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{a_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\}_{j\in J\setminus I}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}\sqcap\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus J}

and similarly

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}=\mathtt{p}\&\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I\setminus J}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{a_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{\prime\prime\prime}\}_{j\in J\setminus I}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I\cap J}

Since merge is defined inductively, if
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
(resp.
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
) is defined then
all
\mathtt{L}_{i}\sqcap\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
(resp.
\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}
) are defined for
i\in I\setminus J
. Therefore we can apply induction on the continuations obtaining

(11)

\mathtt{L}_{i}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}\qquad\forall i\in I\cap J

Using (
9
), (
10
) and (
11
) as premises for ‘
<:
’ (first rule) we obtain the thesis
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
for this case.

If
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}=\mathtt{p}\oplus_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}
the thesis is immediate by induction since

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}=\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
.

Inductive cases - MC rules.

The cases for MC, where
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
have the same type of mixed choice (second, fourth and fifth rules of ‘
<:
’ in
Section

F.1
) is also immediate by induction. The case for the third rule also follows by induction observing
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}
and
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}
have the same MC configurations, and so do
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{2}
and
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
, and also observing that the definition of merge does not alter such configuration, being the identity (first rule of merge).

Inductive cases - recursion rules.

If
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}=\mu\mathtt{.}\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}
we have two cases. If
\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}=\mu\mathtt{.}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
then the thesis is by induction using the first rule in the third line (
Section

F.1
).
If unfolding is needed then the rule applied to derive
\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}
is

\frac{\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}}{\mathtt{L}_{i}^{1}=\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}}

By hypothesis
\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
is defined and hence by
Proposition

F.6

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]
is defined. By induction, therefore,

(12)

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}

By
Proposition

F.7
,

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]=\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]
hence (
12
) becomes

(13)

\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{i}^{2}\sqcap\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}

By applying
13
as a premise for the second rule in the third row (
Section

F.1
) we obtain the thesis.
∎

Lemma F.9 (Send-project).

Let
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright=(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y
and assume that

(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}

Lemma F.10 (Stale).

Let
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright=(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y
and assume that

(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma),Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma^{\prime}),Y^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}
. Then the following hold:

\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi,\mathtt{L})\quad\text{iff}\quad\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\sigma(\mathtt{p})\cdot(a,\pi)]

Namely, projection includes in the queue all and only messages that are not stale.

Proof.

Observe that the last message sent is at the tail of the queue
\sigma(\mathtt{p})
. By induction on the derivation of the local transition, proceeding by case analysis on the last rule applied.

Base case - axiom
\mathtt{[Snd]}

In this case we have no MC context. So

\mathtt{G}
has the form of an interaction from
\mathtt{p}
to
\mathtt{q}
(either top level or as continuation of other interactions from different participants).
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{p}=\mathtt{q}\oplus\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{q}=\mathtt{p}\&\{a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}i}\}_{i\in I}
.
By
\mathtt{[Snd]}

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\sigma(\mathtt{p})\cdot(a,\pi)]
. Since
\pi=0
we have trivially
\neg\mathtt{stale}(0,\mathtt{L})
hence done.

Inductive cases

There are four send rules in the local semantics:
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
,
\mathtt{[LCtxt]}
,
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
, and
\mathtt{[RCtxt]}
. The cases for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
and
\mathtt{[LCtxt]}
– send action on the left – proceed similarly, because the local type in the configuration of
\mathtt{p}
(i.e., whether sender
\mathtt{p}
is committed in the outmost MC) is not relevant to the proof. Similarly
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
and
\mathtt{[RCtxt]}
proceed in the same way for send action on the right. We only show the cases for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
, and
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
. Without loss of generality assume that
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\blacktriangleright\mathtt{G}_{2}
and
\mathtt{L}
is a MC.

If the local action is by
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
then we can infer
\pi=\texttt{l}.\pi^{\prime}
for some
\pi^{\prime}
, and also that
\mathtt{p}
’s local type is a MC. By inspection of the global rules and hypothesis we can infer
\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
for some
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
. Now
\mathtt{L}
can have one of the following forms:

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\blacktriangleright\mathtt{L}_{2}
. Let
\sigma_{L}
and
\sigma_{R}
be the queues obtained by projecting
\mathtt{G}_{1}
and
\mathtt{G}_{2}
, on
\mathtt{q}
. Recall that actions on the RHS are always committing, and that
\mathtt{p}
must not be committed to the RHS when making a LHS step, hence
\sigma_{R}=\epsilon
.
By projection (active MC, third case):

(14)

\sigma=\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R}=\sigma_{L}\quad\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}^{\prime}\circ\sigma_{R}=\sigma_{L}^{\prime}\quad\text{(with $\sigma_{L}^{\prime}$ projection of $\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}$ on $\mathtt{q}$ and $\sigma_{R}=\epsilon$)}

By induction
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{1})
iff
\sigma_{L}^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\sigma_{L}(\mathtt{p})\cdot(a,\pi)]
which gives the thesis by observing that
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{1})
iff
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\texttt{l}.\pi\textquoteright,\mathtt{L})
by definition of
\mathtt{stale}
.

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\blacktriangleright\bullet
.
Again, let
\sigma_{L}
be the queue obtained by projecting
\mathtt{G}_{1}
on
\mathtt{q}
.
By the projection (active MC, second case)
\sigma=\sigma_{L}
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}^{\prime}
(for
\sigma_{L}^{\prime}
projection of
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
on
\mathtt{q}
). By induction
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{1})
iff
\sigma_{L}^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi),\sigma_{L}(\mathtt{p})]
which gives the thesis via observing
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{1})
iff
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\texttt{l}.\pi\textquoteright,\mathtt{L})
by definition of
\mathtt{stale}
.

•

\mathtt{L}=\bullet\blacktriangleright\mathtt{L}_{2}
. In this case
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\texttt{l}.\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L})
which yields the
if
direction. For the
only-if
case we need to show
\sigma^{\prime}\neq\sigma[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi)\cdot\sigma(\mathtt{p})]
which follows by
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{R}=\sigma
. Namely
(a,\pi)
is not in
\sigma^{\prime}
.

If the action is by
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
then
\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
and
\pi=\texttt{r}.\pi^{\prime}
. We have three cases.

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\blacktriangleright\mathtt{L}_{2}
. We have
\sigma=\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R}
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R}^{\prime}
for some
\sigma_{R}^{\prime}
resulting from projection of
\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
on
\mathtt{r}
. In this case, however, it can be that both
\sigma_{L}
and
\sigma_{R}^{\prime}
are non empty. By induction
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{2})
iff
\sigma_{R}^{\prime}=\sigma_{R}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi)\cdot\sigma_{R}(p)]
.
The thesis follows from observing: (a)
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L}_{2})
iff
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\texttt{r}.\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L})
(by definition of
\mathtt{stale}
), (b)
\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R}^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi)\cdot\sigma_{R}(p)]
(by induction) which by definition of
\circ
(which is not commutative and concatenates the messages of
\sigma_{R}(\mathtt{p})

after
those of
\sigma_{L}(\mathtt{p})
we have
\sigma_{L}\circ(\sigma_{R}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi)\cdot\sigma_{R}(p)])=(\sigma_{L}\circ\sigma_{R})[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a,\pi)\cdot\sigma_{L}(p)\circ\sigma(R)(\mathtt{p})]=\sigma^{\prime}
as required.

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\blacktriangleright\bullet
. This case is symmetric to the corresponding (third) case for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
but observing
\neg\mathtt{stale}(\texttt{r}.\pi^{\prime},\mathtt{L})
for the
if
direction and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma_{L}=\sigma
(the new message is not in
\sigma^{\prime}
).

•

\mathtt{L}=\bullet\blacktriangleright\mathtt{L}_{2}
is symmetric to case (2) for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
.

∎

F.3.
Bottom-up fidelity

See
4.1

Proof.

Property (2) follows from
Lemma

F.2
(projected systems have no stale messages). We focus on property (1).

Let
\pi\vdash\mathtt{G}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}
for all
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
.
We also set
Y_{\mathtt{r}}=(\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}})
and
Y_{\mathtt{G}}=\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})}
.
The move by
Y
is by
\mathtt{[Par]}
or
\mathtt{[Low]}
. The case for
\mathtt{[Discard]}
is not possible since
\ell\neq\rho
. The case for
\rho
actions is handled in (2).

Without loss of generality we We focus on

In case of
\mathtt{[Par]}
, and then one of the rules for configurations in Figure
5
,
we reason by induction on the syntax of
\mathtt{G}
.

1.
Communication

Assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{p}\rightarrow\mathtt{q}:\{a_{i}:\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and, without loss of generality,

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=Y_{\mathtt{p}},Y_{\mathtt{q}},Y_{R}

where

\begin{array}[]{ccl}Y_{\mathtt{p}}&=&(\mathtt{p},\penalty\ \mathtt{\mathtt{}}q\mathop{\oplus_{i\in I}}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}i},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\\
Y_{\mathtt{q}}&=&(\mathtt{q},\penalty\ {\mathtt{p}}\&_{i\in I}{a_{i}}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}i},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\epsilon])\\
Y_{R}&=&\{(\mathtt{r},\penalty\ \sqcap_{i\in I}\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{r}})\}_{\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\{\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\}}\end{array}

noting that
\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}(\mathtt{p})
is empty (
Proposition

F.4
). Without loss of generality, the transition
Y_{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\
is by: (1) a send action by
Y_{\mathtt{p}}

8
8

8

Role
\mathtt{q}
cannot move and its first action needs to be the reception of the message from
\mathtt{p}
since local types are mono-threaded by construction)
, or (2) an action by a role
\mathtt{r}
in
Y_{R}
, or (3) a MC instantiation by a configuration in
Y_{R}
.

In case (1) the last rule applied (after
[Par]
) is
[LSnd]
of the local semantics:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y_{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}k},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}),(\mathtt{q},\penalty\ {\mathtt{p}}\&_{i\in I}{a_{i}}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}i},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi^{\prime})]),Y_{R}

for some path
\pi^{\prime}
.
By rule
\mathtt{[Snd]}
of the global semantics

\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ {\mathtt{p}\rightsquigarrow\mathtt{q}:k\{a_{i}:\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}

The projection of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
on role
\mathtt{p}
is

(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}k},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})
, the projection on role
\mathtt{q}
is
(\mathtt{q},{\mathtt{p}}\&_{i\in I}{a_{i}}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}i},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi^{\prime})]
, and the projection on the roles of the configurations in
Y_{R}
is unchanged. Note that the definition of merge (for each role
\mathtt{r}
in
Y_{R}
is not affected by the selection of branch
k
by
\mathtt{p}
).

Consider now case (2) where the action is by a configuration
(\mathtt{r},\,\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}})
in
Y_{R}
with
\mathtt{sbj}(\ell)=\mathtt{r}
.
Let, for all
i\in I
,
\mathtt{G}_{i}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}
. Observe
\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}=\sqcap_{i\in I}\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i}
and by projection

(15)

\forall i,j\in I.\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}=\sigma_{\mathtt{r}j}

We will therefore denote any
\sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}
as
\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}
.
The transition has the following form:

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=(\mathtt{r},\,\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}),(\mathtt{t},\,\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{t}},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{t}}),Y^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{r},\,\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime}),(\mathtt{t},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{t}},\sigma_{\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}),Y^{\prime\prime}

Note that if
\ell
is a send action (assume to is from role
\mathtt{t}
without loss of generality) then
\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}=\sigma_{\mathtt{r}^{\prime}}
, and if
\ell
is a receive action (assume it is from role
\mathtt{t}
without loss of generality) then

\sigma_{\mathtt{t}}=\sigma_{\mathtt{t}^{\prime}}
.

By induction, for all
i\in I

(16)

\mathtt{G}_{i}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{i}^{\prime}

and

(17)

\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i}^{\prime},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i}^{\prime},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}^{g}<:\mathtt{G}_{i}^{\prime}\upharpoonright{\mathtt{r}}

By (
15
) follows that (
17
) is equivalent to

(18)

\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i}^{\prime},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}i}^{g}<:\mathtt{G}_{i}^{\prime}\upharpoonright{\mathtt{r}}

and by
Proposition

F.5
we have that (
18
) is equivalent to

(19)

\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i}^{\prime},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}i},\,\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{g}<:\mathtt{G}_{i}^{\prime}\upharpoonright{\mathtt{r}}

By using (
16
) as a premise in rule
\mathtt{[Cont1]}
of the global semantics we obtain

\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}

It remains to show the second part of the thesis:

(20)

Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y_{g}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\hskip-2.84526pt\upharpoonright

We decompose the reasoning, considering each configuration in
Y^{\prime}
:

•

for role
\mathtt{r}
we need to show

(\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ <:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}

The first part (garbage collection)

(\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{g})

follows by (
19
) and
Proposition

F.5
.
The second part (preorder)

(\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}^{g})<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}

follows by using (
17
) as hypothesis
Proposition

F.8
(merge with ‘
<:
’).

•

For
\mathtt{t}
, the thesis follows again by induction and
Proposition

F.5
, observing that all
\mathtt{G}_{i}^{\prime}\upharpoonright\mathtt{t}
are the same for all
i\in I
.

•

For all other roles the thesis holds by hypothesis since the local types are unchanged, and the queues as well.

In case (3) if MC is an instantiation action, the thesis is by induction.

2.
Message in transit

Assume

\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}}{{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}
}{{{{}{}{{}}

and, without loss of generality,

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=Y_{\mathtt{q}},Y_{R}\qquad Y_{\mathtt{q}}=(\mathtt{q},\penalty\ {\mathtt{p}}\&_{i\in I}{a_{i}}.\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}i},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi^{\prime})])

There are two cases: (1) the transition of
Y_{\mathtt{G}}
is a receive action by
Y_{\mathtt{q}}
or (2) an action by one other configuration in
Y_{R}
.

In case (1) we have by
[LRcv]
:

Y_{\mathtt{q}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}k},\penalty\ \sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\epsilon])=Y_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime}

and by
[Par]

Y_{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ Y_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime},Y_{R}

By rule
\mathtt{[Rcv]}
of the global semantics

\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{q}:k\,\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{k}

It remains to show the second part of the thesis:

(21)

Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y_{g}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\hskip-2.84526pt\upharpoonright

We decompose the reasoning, considering each configuration in
Y^{\prime}
:

•

For
\mathtt{q}
, by induction

\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}k},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\rho}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}k},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}^{g}<:\mathtt{G}_{k}\upharpoonright\mathtt{q}

which directly yields the thesis.

•

The cases for other configurations are straightforward by hypothesis as
Y_{R}
is nto changed.

Case (2) where a configuration in
Y_{R}
makes a step in the continuations
\mathtt{G}_{i}
proceeds analogously to case (2) for communications.

4.
MC definition

Assume

\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
and instantiation is triggered by configuration of role
\mathtt{r}_{1}
in
Y_{\mathtt{G}}
.
Observe that by projection
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{0}
and

\mathtt{L}_{1}=\mathtt{L}^{1}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{1}
with

(22)

\pi\vdash\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{r}_{1}=\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1},\sigma_{0}

By the form of
\mathtt{L}
the transition can only be by rule
[New]
of the local semantics:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{r}1,\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{1},\sigma_{0}),Y_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{1},\sigma_{0}),Y_{2}

By rule
\mathtt{[Inst]}
of the global semantics

\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ {\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}

The projection of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is as follows:

\pi\vdash{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}_{1}=\mathtt{L}_{1}^{1}\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{1},\sigma_{0}

where (
22
) was used to develop the projections of
\mathtt{G}_{1}
and
\mathtt{G}_{2}
on
\mathtt{p}
, to derive
\sigma_{0}
as
\sigma_{0}\circ\sigma_{0}
as desired for
Y_{1}
.
To conclude this case we need to consider
Y=Y_{1},Y_{2}
. Observe that
Y_{2}
is unchanged by the transition, that
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}_{2}=\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{2},\sigma_{0}
and that

\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}^{2}_{2}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}^{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{2}
as desired. This case, when only one configuration instantiate a MC – not immediately paired with the other ones, unlike the global case, justifies the need for the preorder
<:
in the statement.

4.
Active MC

In this case
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
.
Without loss of generality we consider three cases in which: (1)
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is not in
\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{L}
, (2)
\mathtt{r}_{1}\in\mathcal{L}
, and (3)
\mathtt{r}_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
.

In (1),
Y_{1}=(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11}\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1})
and
Y_{2}=(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})
.
We first consider the case where the last rule applied is on the left. We show the case for
[LSnd]
. The cases for
[LRcv1]

and
[LRcv2]
proceed similarly (but with no need of garbage collection - the only interesting but is observing that commitment in global types reflects directly on local types by projection) whereas
[LCtxt]
is straightforward by induction.

[LSnd]
for some path
\pi
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi^{\prime}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11}\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\\
\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11}^{\prime}\blacktriangleright^{c}\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto\sigma_{2}\cdot(a_{k},\pi)])\end{array}

with premise

(23)

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi^{\prime}.\texttt{l}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\\
\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi)])\end{array}

Observe that
\pi
may refer to a MC that is nested inside
c
.

By induction on the premise
(\ref{eq:prem11})
we have that

\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}

and the projection of
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
on role
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is

\mathtt{L}_{11}^{\prime\prime},\sigma_{1}
with
\mathtt{L}_{11}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{11}^{\prime\prime}
.

The assumption for this case (1), that
\mathtt{r}_{1}\not\in\mathcal{R}
, satistfies the premise of rule
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
of the global semantics, which we can then apply:

\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}

It remains to show the thesis for the projection of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
on
\mathtt{r}_{2}
.

Recall
(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})
moves to
(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi)])
.
By
Lemma

F.9
the projected local type from
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
on
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is
\mathtt{L}_{2}
itself. So,
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright\mathtt{r}_{2}=\mathtt{L}_{2},\,\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
for some
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
.
It remains to show that
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathsf{gc}(\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi)])
that is, the new message
(a_{k},\pi)
is in the projected queue if and only if it is not garbage collected (i.e., not stale), which follows by
Lemma

F.10
.

If
Y
moves on the RHS we have three possible rules:
[RSnd]
or
[RRcv]
or
[RCtxt]
. We show
[RSnd]
below.

Let
Y_{2}=(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})
. By
[RSnd]
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi^{\prime}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{11}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\\
\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{r}_{1},\bullet\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto\sigma_{2}\cdot(a_{k},\pi)])\end{array}

with premise

(24)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\pi^{\prime}.\texttt{r}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2})\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\\
\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{r}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}),(\mathtt{r}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma_{2}[\mathtt{r}_{1}\mapsto(a_{k},\pi)])\end{array}

By induction on the premise
(\ref{eq:prem1})
we have

\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}

and the projection of
\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
on role
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is

\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime\prime},\sigma_{1}
with
\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime\prime}
.

Since
\mathtt{r}_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
then
\mathtt{G}_{2}
cannot make action
\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}
hence we can apply
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
of the global semantics obtaining:

\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{r}_{1}\mathtt{r}_{2}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}\cup\{\mathtt{r}_{1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}=\mathtt{G}^{\prime}

The projection of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
on
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is

\bullet\blacktriangleright^{{\iota}}\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime\prime},\sigma_{1}
since
\mathtt{r}_{1}
is committed to the RHS, with the projection of the LHS given by induction. Also by induction
\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime\prime}
hence by definition of ‘
<:
’ we have
\bullet\blacktriangleright^{{\iota}}\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime}<:\bullet\blacktriangleright^{{\iota}}\mathtt{L}_{12}^{\prime\prime}
as desired for
\mathtt{r}_{1}
. The thesis for
\mathtt{r}_{2}
follows by
Lemma

F.9
(the local type is unchanged) and
Lemma

F.10
(the queue of the reached state is the projected queue modulo garbage collection).

Case (2) is similar to case (1) but simpler, since only LHS moves are possible by
\mathtt{r}_{1}
. In case (3) we have
Y_{1}=(\mathtt{r}_{1},\bullet\blacktriangleright^{{\iota}}\mathtt{L}_{12},\sigma_{1})
hence the last rule applied is
[RCtxt]
and the thesis is straightforward by induction.

5.
Recursion

Directly by induction.
∎

F.4.
Top-Down fidelity

See
4.2

Proof.

Lemma

4.1
follows directly from the more general property for transitions with general
\pi
environments.
Let
\pi\vdash\mathtt{G}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}}
for all
\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
.
We also set
Y_{\mathtt{r}}=(\mathtt{r},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{r}},\sigma_{\mathtt{r}})
and
Y_{\mathtt{G}}=\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})}
.

We proceed by induction on the transition of
\mathtt{G}
, reasoning by case analysis on the last rule used.

\mathtt{[Snd]}
Assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. By projection

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\oplus_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}),(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{p}\&_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}),Y_{r}

with
Y_{r}
being the configuration of participants in
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\{\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\}
.

By
\mathtt{[Snd]}
of the global semantics, for a
k\in I
,

\mathtt{p}\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{q}:\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
