\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{q}:k\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}

By one application of rule
[Par]
of the local semantics with one application of
[Snd]
as a premise:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\epsilon:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{q}\oplus_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}),(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{p}\&_{i\in I}a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i},\sigma_{q}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\vec{m}]),Y_{r}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ Y_{p}^{\prime},Y_{q}^{\prime},Y_{r}\end{array}

with

.
The projection of
\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

By
\mathtt{[Rcv]}
of the global semantics,

\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{q}:k\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{k}

By one application of rule
[Par]
of the local semantics with one application of
[Snd]
as a premise:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\epsilon:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y_{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{k},\sigma_{q}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\vec{m}]),(\mathtt{r},\sqcap_{i\in I}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}_{i},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}),Y_{p},Y_{r}\end{array}

Note that
Y_{\mathtt{r}}
is unchanged (as all other roles other than
\mathtt{q}
. The projection of
\mathtt{G}_{k}
on
\mathtt{p}
is still
Y_{p}=\upharpoonright{\epsilon}{\mathtt{G}_{k}}{\mathtt{p}}
. The projection of
\mathtt{G}_{k}
on
\mathtt{q}
is indeed the state reached by
\mathtt{q}
, that is
(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{k},\sigma_{q}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\vec{m}])
with
\sigma_{k}=\sigma_{q}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\vec{m}]
. As to all other
\mathtt{r}
, their projection is now
\upharpoonright{\epsilon}{\mathtt{G}_{k}}{\mathtt{r}}=\mathtt{L}_{k},\sigma_{k}
. But

(25)

\sqcap_{i\in I}\mathtt{L}_{i}<:\mathtt{L}_{k}

hence done.
Observe that (
25
) holds: (1) by ‘
<:
’ (second rule) if
\mathtt{r}
has a receive/branching prefix, (2) otherwise by
\sqcap_{i\in I}\mathtt{L}_{i}=\mathtt{L}_{k}
.

\mathtt{[Cont1]}
,
\mathtt{[Cont2]}
, and
\mathtt{[Rec]}
are straightforward by induction.

\mathtt{[Inst]}
We can assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}
. By projection

(26)

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})}

By
\mathtt{[Inst]}
of the global semantics

\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}

with

\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{c}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\upharpoonright\penalty\ =\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})\}_{{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})}}

A configuration in
Y
that has been projected can either have MC
c
to instantiate top level or after some communication actions. Let
J\subseteq\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
be the roles whose configurations have the MC
c
instantiated by
\mathtt{G}
at top level, and
m=|J|
. With
m
applications of rule

[Par]
with
[New]
of the local semantics as premise:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\epsilon:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y_{\mathtt{G}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\vv{\nu}}}\penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})\}_{{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap J}}\cup\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})\}_{{\mathtt{p}\in J}}\end{array}

The thesis follows by observing that for all
\mathtt{p}\in J
,

(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})<:(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c^{\prime}}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{0})
.

\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
Assume
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{{c},{n}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
. By projection we have two cases: either
\mathcal{L}
is empty or not.

First, assume
\mathcal{L}\neq\emptyset
. Since
\mathtt{G}
is reachable from an aware and balanced state (hypothesis), it is coherent (Lemma
D.12
) and hence
\mathcal{R}=\emptyset
. Without loss of generality assume

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\bullet,\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{L}}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L}}}

where
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})
is partitioned in the set of committed roles (in
\mathcal{L}
), and not committed roles (not in
\mathcal{L}
).
By
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
of the global semantics (omitting
c,n
),

(27)

\frac{\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}}{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}

Focussing on the LHS blocks of the local types in
Y_{\mathtt{G}}
:

Y_{1}=\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{L}}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L}}

By induction, looking at the premise in (
27
)

Y_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\vv{\nu c}}}\penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{L}}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L}}

where for all
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L}
,
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}=\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}\circ\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
for some
\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
.
Moreover,

(28)

\forall\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{L},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \texttt{l}\vdash\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{p}=\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}

and

(29)

\forall\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \texttt{l}\vdash\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{p}=\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}

We need to check that the projection of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
is
Y^{\prime}
.
The thesis for roles committed on the LHS is straightforward by induction, as shown in (
28
). The case for
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{L}
follows by observing that: (1)
projection of
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
into
\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}
follows by induction as shown in (
29
); (2)
\mathtt{L}_{2}
is unchanged hence projection of
\mathtt{G}_{2}^{\prime}
into
\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
follows by hypothesis and projection.
The queues are unchanged hence still
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}=\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}\circ\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
.

The case for
\mathcal{L}=\emptyset
is symmetric, with possibly some role committed on the RHS:

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=\{(\mathtt{p},\bullet\,\blacktriangleright\,\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}\,,\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{R}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R}}

By
\mathtt{[Ctx1]}
of the global semantics,

\frac{\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu c}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}}{\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{{c},{n}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\nu{\iota}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}^{{c},{n}}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}

By induction,
(where
Y_{c}
are the configurations of roles in
\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\cap\mathcal{R}
– already committed on the right):

(30)

Y_{1}=\{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R}}\cup Y_{c}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\vv{\nu c}}}\penalty\ \{(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}})\}_{\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R}}\cup Y_{c}

where for all
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R}
,
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}=\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}\circ\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
for some
\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
.

By induction,

(31)

\forall\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \texttt{l}\vdash\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\upharpoonright}\mathtt{p}=\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{p}}^{\prime},\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}

The thesis for
\mathtt{p}\in\mathtt{R}(\mathtt{G})\setminus\mathcal{R}
follows from observing that projection is defined inductively on the two sides and: (1) (
31
) and (
30
) for the LHS block; (2) the global and local types are unchanged hence the thesis for the RHS follows by hypothesis.
The queues are unchanged hence still
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}=\sigma_{1\mathtt{p}}\circ\sigma_{2\mathtt{p}}
.

\mathtt{[LSnd]}
Let
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
.
By
\mathtt{[LSnd]}

(32)

\frac{\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}}{\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}

We have two cases: (a)
\mathtt{p}
is uncommitted, (b)
\mathtt{p}
is committed.

Case (a): If
\mathtt{p}
is uncommitted, let
Y_{1}
be the system obtained by projecting
\mathtt{G}_{1}
on all its roles.
By induction, by the premise in (
32
) it follows, observing that the definition of ‘
<:
’ for systems ignores the queues (see
Section

F.1
):

(33)

Y_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}!a_{k}}}\penalty\ Y_{1}^{\prime}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \land\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ Y_{1}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\upharpoonright

From
Equation

33
we obtain (via definition of ’
<:
’)
Y_{1}^{\prime}\blacktriangleright Y_{2}<:Y^{\prime}
.
Lookinq at the queues now, without loss of generality, assume the only queue that changed in the transition from
Y
to
Y^{\prime}
is
\mathtt{q}
’s. Denoting the queue of
\mathtt{q}
in
Y
as
\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}
and the queue of
\mathtt{q}
in
Y^{\prime}
as
\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime}
we have

\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime}=\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}[\mathtt{p}\mapsto\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}(\mathtt{p})\cdot(a_{k},\pi)]
.
The queues of all other roles are unchanged.
By
Lemma

F.2
(garbageless projections)

Y
’s queues have no stale messages. Hence the only message that may cause the queues of
Y^{\prime}
be different from thos of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
is
(a_{k},\pi)
. By
Lemma

F.10
we have that
(a_{k},\pi)
is stale (can be garbage collected) if and only if it is not in in the queues of
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
, as desired.

Case (b) is essentially as case (a) except the projection of
\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}
ignores the right-hand side process.

\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
Let
\mathtt{G}=\mathtt{G}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}
.
By
\mathtt{[LRcv1]}

\frac{\mathtt{G}_{1}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\qquad\mathtt{q}\not\in\mathcal{R}\land(\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{L}\lor\mathtt{q}=\mathtt{r})}{\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?a_{k}}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright\mkern 1.0mu}_{\mathcal{L}\cup\{\mathtt{q}\},\mathcal{R}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{G}_{2}}

Without loss of generality,

(34)

Y_{\mathtt{G}}=Y_{\mathtt{q}},Y_{rest}

where either (a)
Y_{\mathtt{q}}=(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{q}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{q}},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}})
or (b)
Y_{q}=(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{q}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\bullet,\sigma_{\mathtt{q}})
. In either case the thesis follows by projection where the LHS of the outmost MC is the projection of
\mathtt{G}_{1}^{\prime}
by induction, whereas the RHS of the MC is just left out by the second projection rule for active MC.
In the case for
\mathtt{[LRcv2]}
either
\mathtt{q}
is already committed or remains uncommitted. In the first case,
Y_{\mathtt{q}}=(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{q}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\bullet,\sigma_{\mathtt{q}})
and in the second case
(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{1\mathtt{q}}\,\blacktriangleright\,\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{q}},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}})
.
The case is similar to
\mathtt{[LRcv1]}
, except the action is not a committing action hence
Y_{\mathtt{q}}
and
Y_{\mathtt{q}}^{\prime}
have the same form.

The case for
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
is symmetric to the case for
\mathtt{[LSnd]}
but simpler: no action can happen on the right hand side if
\mathcal{L}
is not empty so there is no need for garbage collection.
The case for
\mathtt{[RRcv]}
is as
\mathtt{[RSnd]}
since on the right-hand side both send and receive are committing.
∎

F.5.
Lemmata for
Theorem

4.4

Theorem 4.4 requires to show that the following relation is a weak correspondence (
Definition

4.3
).

(35)

R=\{(\mathtt{G},Y)\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathtt{G}\text{ is reachable from an initial, aware, balanced global type }.\penalty\ Y<:\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\}

Lemma

4.2
and
Lemma

4.1
gives us only one step of correspondence when
Y=\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
. We need to show the transitive closure holds with
Y<:\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
.

Given a pair
(\mathtt{G},Y)\in R
, we use the notation
\mathtt{G}\approx^{(1)}Y
to denote condition (1) in
Definition

4.3
and
\mathtt{G}\approx^{(2)}Y
to denote condition (2) in
Definition

4.3
. The fact that
R
is a weak correspondence follows from
Lemma

F.12
and
Lemma

F.15
.

Lemma F.11.

If
\mathtt{L}<:\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
and
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime},\sigma)
then
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime\prime},\sigma)
and
\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

(sketch)
The proof is by induction on the derivation of
\mathtt{L}<:\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
proceeding by case analysis on the last rules in
Section

F.1
. The base cases are trivial as
\mathtt{end}
and
\mathtt{t}
do not make any transition.
The case for

\frac{\mathtt{L}_{k}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{k}\quad k\in I}{\mathtt{p}\,\&_{i\mathclose{\in}I}\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{p}\,\&\,a_{k}.\mathtt{L}_{k}}

follows by induction since
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}=\mathtt{p}\,\&_{i\mathclose{\in}I}\,a_{i}.\mathtt{L}_{i}^{\prime}

has more action options than
\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{p}\,\&\,a_{k}.\mathtt{L}_{k}
and
\mathtt{L}_{k}<:\mathtt{L}_{k}^{\prime}
by inductive definition of ‘
<:
’.

The cases for rule

\frac{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{1}\qquad\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}_{2}}{\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}\penalty\ \mathbin{<:}\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}}

with
\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\blacktriangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}
follows by observing that the two terms in the conclusion can perform the same actions modulo a MC instantiation (i.e.,
\tau
) that
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mkern 1.0mu\triangleright^{c}\mkern 1.0mu}\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
can always perform by local semantic rule
[New]
. Similarly for the rule below, by action
[Rec]
.

\frac{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}^{\prime}/\mathtt{t}]\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}}{\mu\mathtt{t}.\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\mathbin{<:}\mathtt{L}}

All other rules are straightforward by induction as left and right-hand side terms of ‘
<:
’ in the conclusions are the same.
∎

Lemma F.12.

If
(\mathtt{G},Y)\in R
then

\mathtt{G}\approx^{(1)}Y
.

Proof.

By
R
we have
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\penalty\ <:Y
. If
\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}
by
Lemma

4.2

\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime}
and
Y^{\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
.

We extend
Lemma

F.11
to configurations: observe that
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
and
Y
can be decomposed into a finite number of configurations by rule
\mathtt{[PSystem]}
, recalled below for convenience with
Y=\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{2}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in P}
and
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright=\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{1}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in P}
.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\forall\mathtt{r}\mathop{\in}P\penalty\ \penalty\ Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{2}\mathbin{<:}Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{1}}{\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{2}\}_{\mathtt{r}\in P}\mathbin{<:}\{Y_{\mathtt{r}}^{1}\}_{\mathtt{r}\mathclose{\in}P}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathtt{[PSystem]}\end{array}

By applying
Lemma

F.11
to the pairs of corresponding (same participant) configurations, by hypothesis

\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\penalty\ <:Y
we obtain
Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}<:Y^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence, by transitivity of ‘
<:
’ we have
Y^{\prime}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
as desired.
∎

Lemma F.13.

If
\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}
,
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime},\sigma)
, and
\ell
is not a receive action, then

(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime},\sigma)
and
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

First, we show that
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\
by induction on the derivation
\mathtt{L}_{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}
proceeding by case analysis on the last rule used.

Since
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L},\sigma)
does not make a receive action, then the last rule used cannot be a receive/branching (first rule where
\,\pic@makebox@{\framebox}{framed=true}(3.0,6.0){}\,=\&
and second rule in
Section

F.1
). By inspection of the remaining rules, the thesis follows by inductive hypothesis. Action
\ell
can be mimicked immediately unless
\mathtt{L}
is a MC definition, in which case it is mimicked after a MC initialization (i.e., a
\tau
action).
The fact that the continuation is still in a preorder relation is given by
\mathtt{L}<:\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
and by inductive definition of ‘
<:
’.
∎

For receiving actions, we have a weaker lemma which follows mechanically by induction on the derivation of ‘
<:
’.

Lemma F.14.

If
\penalty\ \mathtt{L}_{1}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}
,
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime},\sigma)
with
\ell
a receive action, and
(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ (\mathtt{p},\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime},\sigma)
then
\mathtt{L}_{1}^{\prime}<:\mathtt{L}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Lemma F.15.

If
(\mathtt{G},Y)\in R
then
\mathtt{G}\approx^{(2)}Y
.

Proof.

By hypothesis we have
Y<:\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
.
First, assume
Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime}
and
\ell
is not a receive action.
By
Lemma

F.13
we have
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}<:Y^{\prime\prime}
.
By
Lemma

4.1

\mathtt{G}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathtt{G}^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime\prime}
with
Y^{\prime\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
.
Again, since
Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime}
, by
Lemma

F.13
we have

Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
and
Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}<:Y^{\prime\prime\prime}
. By transitivity of ‘
<:
’ we have
Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}<:\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
as desired.

9
9

9

Note, the step
Y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{}\hskip-3.44444pt^{*}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
is necessary when the role that is supposed to receive the message sent (for now stored in its queue) is getting a message that commits them to the opposite side wrt where the message is received. In this case, the queue of the receiver may include stale messages that need to be purged to restore equality of the queues between
Y^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{G}^{\prime}\upharpoonright
(projected systems never have stale messages).

The case for receive action is interesting.
If
Y\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime}
by a receive action, say
\ell=\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?(a_{k},\pi)
then this action only involves one configuration in
Y
: the configuration of the receiver
(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}_{2\mathtt{q}},\sigma_{2\mathtt{q}})
and
\sigma_{2\mathtt{q}}(\mathtt{p})=\vec{m_{1}}\cdot(a_{k},\pi)\cdot\vec{m_{2}}
.
This case is interesting because the local type of
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{q}
could be of the form
\mathtt{p}\,\&\,a_{j}.\mathtt{L}_{j}^{\prime}
where
\mathtt{q}
is already set to receive a specific label
j
by the second rule of
<:
in
Section

F.1
and this requires us to show that
k=j
for any
k
that
\mathtt{q}
may have chosen in
Y
.

We use hypothesis
Y<:\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
focussing on the configurations
Y_{\mathtt{p}}
of sender
\mathtt{p}
and
Y_{\mathtt{q}}
receiver
\mathtt{q}
. Let
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{p}={\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}
and
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\mathtt{q}={\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}}},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}
. By
\mathtt{[PConfig]}
we have
Y_{\mathtt{p}}<:(\mathtt{p},{\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{p}}},\sigma_{\mathtt{p}})
and
Y_{\mathtt{q}}<:(\mathtt{q},{\mathtt{L}_{\mathtt{q}}},\sigma_{\mathtt{q}})
.

If
(a_{k},\pi)
is in the queue of
\mathtt{q}
in
Y_{\mathtt{q}}
then it is also in
\sigma_{\mathtt{q}}(\mathtt{p})
, which means
\mathtt{p}
has already sent the message
k
in
\mathtt{G}
. More precisely,
\mathtt{G}
must have a subterm which is a message in transit by
\mathtt{p}
to
\mathtt{q}
of label
a_{k}
in the path
\pi
.

So, the branch selected by
\mathtt{p}
in
Y
must be the same as the one selected by
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
yielding
j=k
. Hence, also
\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\mathtt{p}\mathtt{q}?(a_{k},\pi)}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime\prime}
and by
Lemma

F.14

Y^{\prime}<:Y^{\prime\prime}
. This case now proceeds as the one above for non-receiving actions.

∎

Appendix G
Orphan Message Freedom (OMF)

To reason on orphan messages is presence of MC, it helps to define the notion of active context. Intuitively, an active context is a context where the hole is
not
in a stale path.

Definition G.1 (Active Contexts).

We define below global active contexts
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}(\mathtt{p})
.
Global active contexts are parameterized on a role
\mathtt{p}
. Parameterization is necessary as some contexts may be active only for some participants when other participants have already committed to the opposite side. We write
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}
(omitting the parameter for readability) when not relevant or clear from the context.

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}(\mathtt{p})&::=&[]\\

to denote a general global context obtained by the rules for

Definition G.3 (Actions
\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell@\pi}}\penalty\
).

We say that
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}[\mathtt{G}]
moves at
\pi
with label
\ell
, written
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}[\mathtt{G}]\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell@\pi}}\penalty\
if
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}[\mathtt{G}]\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ \mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}[\mathtt{G}^{\prime}]
with
\mathtt{G}\neq\mathtt{G}^{\prime}
and
\pi=\mathtt{Path}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}})=\mathtt{Path}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime})
.

Definition G.4 (Global staleness).

We say that
\pi
is stale in
\mathtt{G}
if there exists no
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}
such that
\mathtt{G}=\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}[\mathtt{G}^{\prime}]
and
\mathtt{Path}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}})=\pi
.

Proposition G.5.

For all
\mathtt{G}=\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}(\mathtt{q})[\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

Proposition G.6 (Stale persistency).

If
Y,(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L},\sigma)\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\xrightarrow{\ell}}\penalty\ Y^{\prime},(\mathtt{q},\mathtt{L}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
and
\mathtt{stale}(\pi,\mathtt{L})
then
\mathtt{stale}(\pi,\mathtt{L}^{\prime})
.

The proof is mechanical by induction on the transition, reasoning by case analysis on the last rule applied. Observe that transitions never remove MC instances and can only change the tree structure of nested MC by either expanding the leaves with new instantiations, or committing hence
adding
(never removing) stale sides
\bullet
.

with

and

as required.

If

then without loss of generality

\mathtt{G}=\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}[\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{q})[\mathtt{p}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\hbox to8.94pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.65598pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 0 L 1.28 1.11 L 3.84 -1.11 L 6.4 1.11 L 7.68 0 L 7.68 0 L 7.7 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.66763pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.49 0 L 0.28 1.88 L 0 0 L 0.28 -1.88 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\mathtt{q}:k\,\{a_{i}.\mathtt{G}_{i}\}_{i\in I}]]

where
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{q})
is the largest context with only send/receive actions, and since by definition it has no MC it is active. We now use the form of
\pi
to decompose
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}
and show it is active. By inspection of the projection rules (by either fourth rule, first and third case) we have observe that
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}
has the following form:

\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}={\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}}^{\prime}[\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{q})\blacktriangleright_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\mathtt{G}_{2}]

If the fourth projection rule - first case - was used to project
\mathtt{G}
, then
\mathtt{q}\in\mathcal{L}
hence by coherence
\mathtt{q}\not\in\mathcal{R}
. If the fourth rule - third case - was used
again, by side condition
\mathtt{q}\not\in\mathcal{L}\cup\mathcal{R}
we have
\mathtt{q}\not\in\mathcal{R}
. Since in both cases
\mathtt{q}\not\in\mathcal{R}
then
[]\blacktriangleright_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\mathtt{G}_{2}
is active, and hence
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{q})\blacktriangleright_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\mathtt{G}_{2}
is also active, as obtained by plugging an active context into another active one.

By induction, we have
{\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}}^{\prime}
is active for
\mathtt{q}
with path
\pi^{\prime}
. Hence,

\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}
is active because it is obtained by plugging context
\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\prime}(\mathtt{q})\blacktriangleright_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}}\mathtt{G}_{2}
that is active for
\mathtt{p}
with
\pi
into another context
{\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}}^{\circ}}^{\prime}
that is also active for
\mathtt{q}
with
\pi
(this is by definition of active contexts).

The case for
\pi=\pi^{\prime}.\texttt{r}
is symmetric.

∎

Proposition

G.8
follows by generalizing
Proposition

G.7
, by mechanical induction on the length of
\vec{m_{1}}
and on the derivation of
Y<:\mathtt{G}\upharpoonright
.

, with

\pi=\mathtt{Path}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathtt{G}})
as desired.
